\|\nabla\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\|^{2}_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}\lesssim_{m,\varepsilon}\frac{t}{N}.

The proof of Lemma
C.1
is quite involved. So we divide it into several subsections as follows.

C.1
Decomposition of the empirical gradient norm

Recall that

\|\nabla\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\|^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell_{j}=1}^{N}\left[\frac{1}{N^{m-1}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{j-1},\ell_{j+1},\dots,\ell_{m}\leq N}\left(1-p_{\varepsilon}^{*}(X^{(1)}_{\ell_{1}},\dots,X^{(j)}_{\ell_{j}},\dots,X_{\ell_{m}}^{(m)})\right)\right]^{2}.

Denote
Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}=1-p_{\varepsilon}^{*}(X^{(1)}_{\ell_{1}},\dots,X^{(j)}_{\ell_{j}},\dots,X_{\ell_{m}}^{(m)})
and

K_{j}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell_{j}=1}^{N}\left[\frac{1}{N^{m-1}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{j-1},\ell_{j+1},\dots,\ell_{m}\leq N}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right]^{2}.

Then, we can write

\displaystyle\|\nabla\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\|^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell_{j}=1}^{N}\left[\frac{1}{N^{m-1}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{j-1},\ell_{j+1},\dots,\ell_{m}\leq N}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right]^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}K_{j}.

(30)

In the following, we only detail the analysis of term
K_{1}
for simplicity and readability. The terms
K_{j}
,
j=2,\dots,m
can be dealt with in the same way. The next lemma is a crucial observation.

Lemma C.2
.

Under Assumption (
1
)-(
3
), we can bound

\displaystyle K_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N^{m+1}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{m-1}\leq N}\left(\sum_{1\leq\ell_{m}\leq N}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}-\mathbb{E}_{m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{r=3}^{m}\frac{2^{m-r+2}}{N^{r}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{r-2}\leq N}\left(\sum_{1\leq\ell_{r-1}\leq N}\E_{r,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}-\mathbb{E}_{r-1,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right)^{2},

(31)

where
\mathbb{E}_{q,...,m}
means the expectation taken with respect to
\{X^{(j)}_{i}\}_{j\in\{q,\dots,m\},i\in[N]}
.

Lemma
C.3
is needed to prove the Lemma
C.2
. Specifically, the second term in (
31
) comes from recursively applying Lemma
C.3
by induction.

Lemma C.3
(Induction lemma).

Under Assumption (
2
), for any
s\in\{3,\dots,m\}
, we can bound

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1}\leq N}\left(\frac{1}{N^{s-2}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{s-1}\leq N}\E_{s,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N^{s}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{s-2}\leq N}\left(\sum_{1\leq\ell_{s-1}\leq N}\E_{s,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}-\E_{s-1,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right)^{2}

(32)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2}{N}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1}\leq N}\left(\frac{1}{N^{s-3}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{s-2}\leq N}\E_{s-1,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right)^{2}.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{N}\Big(\frac{1}{N^{s-2}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{s-1}\leq N}\E_{s,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{N}\Big(\frac{1}{N^{s-2}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{s-1}\leq N}(\E_{s,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}-\E_{s-1,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{N^{s-3}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{s-2}\leq N}\E_{s-1,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{N}\Big(\frac{1}{N^{s-2}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{s-1}\leq N}(\E_{s,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}-\E_{s-1,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}})\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{2}{N}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{N}\Big(\frac{1}{N^{s-3}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{s-2}\leq N}\E_{s-1,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N^{3}}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{N}\Big(\frac{1}{N^{s-3}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{s-2}\leq N}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{s-1}\leq N}(\E_{s,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}-\E_{s-1,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}})\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{2}{N}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{N}\Big(\frac{1}{N^{s-3}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{s-2}\leq N}\E_{s-1,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N^{s}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{s-2}\leq N}\Big(\sum_{1\leq\ell_{s-1}\leq N}\E_{s,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}-\E_{s-1,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{2}{N}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{N}\Big(\frac{1}{N^{s-3}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{s-2}\leq N}\E_{s-1,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\Big)^{2},

where we use basic inequality for the first inequality and Jensen’s inequality for the second inequality.
∎

Now we are ready to prove Lemma
C.2
.

Observe that

\displaystyle K_{1}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{N}

\displaystyle\left[\frac{1}{N^{m-1}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{m}\leq N}\left(Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}-\mathbb{E}_{m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right)+\frac{1}{N^{m-1}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{m}\leq N}\mathbb{E}_{m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right]^{2},

so we get

\displaystyle K_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{N}\left[\frac{1}{N^{m-1}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{m-1}\leq N}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{m}\leq N}\left(Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}-\mathbb{E}_{m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{N}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{N}\left[\frac{1}{N^{m-1}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{m-1}\leq N}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{m}\leq N}\mathbb{E}_{m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N^{3}}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{N}\left[\frac{1}{N^{m-2}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{m-1}\leq N}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{m}\leq N}\left(Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}-\mathbb{E}_{m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{N}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{N}\left[\frac{1}{N^{m-1}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{m-1}\leq N}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{m}\leq N}\mathbb{E}_{m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N^{m+1}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{m-1}\leq N}\left[\sum_{1\leq\ell_{m}\leq N}\left(Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}-\mathbb{E}_{m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{N}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{N}\left[\frac{1}{N^{m-2}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{m-1}\leq N}\mathbb{E}_{m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right]^{2},

where we use Jensen’s inequality for the second inequality. Moreover, the marginal feasibility constrain (
29
) indicates that

\mathbb{E}_{2,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{m}}=0.

(35)

As a consequence of equation (
35
) and using Lemma
C.3
recursively, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{N}\left[\frac{1}{N^{m-2}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{m-1}\leq N}\mathbb{E}_{m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=3}^{m}\frac{2^{m-s+1}}{N^{s}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{s-2}\leq N}\left(\sum_{1\leq\ell_{s-1}\leq N}E_{s,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}-E_{s-1,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right)^{2}.

(36)

Combining (
C.1
), (
C.1
) and (
36
), the proof of the lemma is done.
∎

C.2
Concentration of the empirical gradient norm

Recall that as proved in Lemma
C.2
,

\displaystyle K_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N^{m+1}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{m-1}\leq N}\left(\sum_{1\leq\ell_{m}\leq N}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}-\mathbb{E}_{m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{r=3}^{m}\frac{2^{m-r+2}}{N^{r}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{r-2}\leq N}\left(\sum_{1\leq\ell_{r-1}\leq N}E_{r,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}-\mathbb{E}_{r-1,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle K_{1,m+1}+\sum_{r=3}^{m}K_{1,r}.

Motivated by this fact, we are going to derive high probability bounds for
K_{1,s}
separately in the following lemmas, for
s\in\{3,\dots,m+1\}
. These bounds together will establish the concentration for
K_{1}
and consequently the empirical gradient norm
\|\nabla\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\|_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}^{2}
. To lighten the notation, we denote
\mathscr{X}_{k}=\{X^{(k)}_{1},\dots,X^{(k)}_{N}\}
for any
k\in[m]
in the following proofs

Lemma C.4
(Concentration of
K_{1,m+1}
).

Under Assumption (
1
)-(
3
), for any
t>0
, with probability more than
1-2e^{-t}
,

K_{1,m+1}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\frac{t}{N}.

(37)

For
1\leq\ell_{m}\leq N
, let

A_{\ell_{m}}=(Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}-\mathbb{E}_{m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}})_{1\leq\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{m-1}\leq N}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{m-1}}.

Conditional on
\prod_{j=1}^{m-1}\mathscr{X}_{j}
,

A_{1},\dots,A_{N}
are independent due to assumption
1
. Also,
\|A_{\ell_{m}}\|_{2}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}N^{\frac{m-1}{2}}
because of assumption
2
and
\mathbb{E}_{m}A_{\ell_{m}}=0
owing to assumption
3
. Note that

\frac{1}{N^{m+1}}\left\|\sum_{\ell_{m}=1}^{N}A_{\ell_{m}}\right\|^{2}_{2}=\frac{1}{N^{m+1}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{m-1}\leq N}\left[\sum_{1\leq\ell_{m}\leq N}\left(Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}-\mathbb{E}_{m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right)\right]^{2}=\frac{1}{2}K_{1,m+1},

using Lemma
J.3
, and we have for any
u>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\sum_{\ell_{m}=1}^{N}A_{\ell_{m}}\right\|\gtrsim_{\varepsilon}u\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{u^{2}}{N\cdot N^{m-1}}\right),

i.e.,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{N^{\frac{m+1}{2}}}\sum_{\ell_{m}=1}^{N}A_{\ell_{m}}\right\|^{2}\gtrsim_{\varepsilon}\frac{t}{N}\right)\leq 2e^{-t}.

(38)

Namely, for any
t>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(K_{1,m+1}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\frac{t}{N}\right)\geq 1-2e^{-t}.

(39)

∎

Lemma C.5
(Concentration of
K_{1,r}
).

Under Assumption (
1
)-(
3
), for any
t>0
,
r\in\{3,\dots,m\}
, with probability exceeding
1-2e^{-t}
,

K_{1,r}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\frac{t}{N}.

(40)

For
1\leq\ell_{r-1}\leq N
, let

B_{\ell_{r-1}}=(\mathbb{E}_{r,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}-\mathbb{E}_{r-1,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}})_{1\leq\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{r-2}\leq N}\in\mathbb{R}^{N^{r-2}}.

Conditional on
\prod_{j=1}^{r-2}\mathscr{X}_{j}
,

B_{1},\dots,B_{N}
are independent on account of assumption
1
. Also,
\|B_{\ell_{r-1}}\|_{2}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}N^{\frac{r-2}{2}}
as a result of assumption
2
and
\mathbb{E}_{r-1,\dots,m}B_{\ell_{r-1}}=0
due to assumption
3
. It is observed that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{r}}\left\|\sum_{\ell_{r-1}=1}^{N}B_{\ell_{r-1}}\right\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{r}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{r-2}\leq N}\left[\sum_{1\leq\ell_{r-1}\leq N}\left(\mathbb{E}_{r,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}-\mathbb{E}_{r-1,\dots,m}Z_{\ell_{1}\dots\ell_{m}}\right)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{m-r+2}}K_{1,r}.

Via Lemma
J.3
, we obtain

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\sum_{\ell_{r-1}=1}^{N}B_{\ell_{r-1}}\right\|\gtrsim_{\varepsilon}u\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{u^{2}}{N\cdot N^{r-2}}\right),

i.e.,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r}{2}}}\sum_{\ell_{r-1}=1}^{N}B_{\ell_{r-1}}\right\|^{2}\gtrsim_{\varepsilon}\frac{t}{N}\right)\leq 2e^{-t}.

(41)

Namely, for any
t>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(K_{1,r}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\frac{t}{N}\right)\geq 1-2e^{-t}.

(42)

∎

C.3
Concluding Lemma
C.1

By (
30
), we know that

\|\nabla\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\|^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}K_{j}.

A combination of Lemma
C.2
, Lemma
C.4
and Lemma
C.5
gives Lemma
C.1
.

D
Concentration of empirical potentials and joint optimal coupling density

We first derive a high probability bound for the empirical multimarginal distributions.

Lemma D.1
(Concentration of empirical multimarginal distributions).

Let
\phi\in L^{\infty}(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{j})
be such that
(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{j})(\phi)=0
. Then, for all
t>0
, we have with probability at least
1-2e^{-t}
,

|(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j})(\phi)|\leq\|\phi\|_{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{2t}{N}}.

We only detail the proof of the inequality for one direction. The other tail follows analogously. For any
\lambda>0
, Chernoff’s bound gives

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{X}}[(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j})(\phi)>t]\leq e^{-\lambda t}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\left[\exp\left\{\lambda(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j})(\phi)\right\}\right].

Observe that
(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j})(\phi)
could be expressed as

(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j})(\phi)=\frac{1}{(N!)^{m-1}}\sum_{\sigma_{2},\dots,\sigma_{m}\in\Sigma_{N}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\phi\left(X^{(1)}_{k},X^{(2)}_{\sigma_{2}(k)},\dots,X^{(m)}_{\sigma_{m}(k)}\right),

where
\Sigma_{N}
is the set of permutations on
N
elements. Combining this fact with Jensen’s inequality gives the bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{X}}[(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j})(\phi)>t]

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\lambda t}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\left[\exp\left\{\frac{\lambda}{(N!)^{m-1}}\sum_{\sigma_{2},\dots,\sigma_{m}\in\Sigma_{N}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\phi(X^{(1)}_{k},X^{(2)}_{\sigma_{2}(k)},\dots,X^{(m)}_{\sigma_{m}(k)})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\lambda t}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\left[\frac{1}{(N!)^{m-1}}\sum_{\sigma_{2},\dots,\sigma_{m}\in\Sigma_{N}}\exp\left\{\frac{\lambda}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\phi(X^{(1)}_{k},X^{(2)}_{\sigma_{2}(k)},\dots,X^{(m)}_{\sigma_{m}(k)})\right\}\right].

Note that for any group of fixed permutations
(\sigma_{2},\dots,\sigma_{m})
, the joint law of
(X^{(1)}_{k},X^{(2)}_{\sigma_{2}(k)},\dots,X^{(m)}_{\sigma_{m}(k)})_{k=1}^{N}
is identical to that of
(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{N})
where
\xi_{k}\sim\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{j}
are independent and identically distributed. Let
\Xi
denote such an i.i.d. sample
(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{N})
. Thus it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\left[\frac{1}{(N!)^{m-1}}\sum_{\sigma_{2},\dots,\sigma_{m}\in\Sigma_{N}}\exp\left\{\frac{\lambda}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\phi(X^{(1)}_{k},X^{(2)}_{\sigma_{2}(k)},\dots,X^{(m)}_{\sigma_{m}(k)})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(N!)^{m-1}}\sum_{\sigma_{2},\dots,\sigma_{m}\in\Sigma_{N}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{X}}\left[\exp\left\{\frac{\lambda}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\phi(X^{(1)}_{k},X^{(2)}_{\sigma_{2}(k)},\dots,X^{(m)}_{\sigma_{m}(k)})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\Xi}\left[\exp\left\{\frac{\lambda}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\phi(\xi_{k})\right\}\right].

Applying Hoeffding’s Lemma and optimizing over
\lambda>0
yields

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{X}}[(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j})(\phi)>t]\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{Nt^{2}}{2\|\phi\|_{\infty}^{2}}\right\}.

∎

Now we provide the precise formulation of the bounded potential property as well as the optimization geometry in the empirical setting, for better clarity. The arguments are almost the same as the population version. Similarly as in the population version, we can define

\widehat{\mathcal{S}}_{L}:=\Big\{\mathbf{f}\in\prod_{j=1}^{m}L^{\infty}(\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}):\|\sum_{j=1}^{m}f_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}(\otimes_{k=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{k})}\leq L,\,\,\hat{\nu}^{N}_{i}(f_{i})=0\,\,\text{for}\,\,i\in[m-1]\Big\},

and compute the gradient

\nabla\hat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}:\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}\to\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}^{\prime}
as

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla\hat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}),\mathbf{g}\right\rangle_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}=\int\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{m}g_{j}\Big)\Big(1-\exp\Big({\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{m}f_{i}-c}{\varepsilon}}\Big)\Big)\Big]d\left(\otimes_{k=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{k}\right),

where
\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}^{\prime}
denotes the dual space of
\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}
. The optimization geometry w.r.t.
\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}
induces a norm of the gradient. For
\mathbf{g}=(g_{1},\dots,g_{m})\in\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}
, we define
\|\mathbf{g}\|_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}:=(\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int g_{j}^{2}d\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j})^{1/2}.

The norm of empirical dual objective gradient could be similarly calculated as

\left\|\nabla\hat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f})\right\|_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int\Big[\int 1-\exp\Big({\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{m}f_{i}-c}{\varepsilon}}\Big)d\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-j}(x_{-j})\Big]^{2}d\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}.

(43)

Proposition D.2
(Bounded empirical dual potentials).

The optimal empirical dual potential
\hat{\bm{f}}^{*}
satisfies that

\max_{j\in[m]}\|\hat{f}^{*}_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq(3m^{2}-5m+3)\|c\|_{\infty}.

where
\|\hat{f}^{*}_{j}\|_{\infty}:=\|\hat{f}^{*}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}(\nu_{j})}
and
\|c\|_{\infty}:=\|c\|_{L^{\infty}(\otimes_{i=1}^{m}\nu_{i})}
.

The same argument as in the proof of Proposition
2.2
implies that

\max_{j\in[m]}\|\hat{f}^{*}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}(\hat{\nu}_{j}^{N})}\leq 3\|c\|_{\infty}.

At the same time, note that the canonical extension defines
that for all
x_{j}\in\mathcal{X}_{j},\,j\in[m]
,

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{j}^{*}(x_{j})

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\log\left\{\int\exp\left(\frac{\sum_{i\neq j}\hat{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})-c(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})}{\varepsilon}\right)d\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-j}(x_{-j})\right\}

\displaystyle\geq-\|c\|_{\infty}-\varepsilon\log\left\{\int\exp\left(\frac{\sum_{i\neq j}\hat{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})}{\varepsilon}\right)d\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-j}(x_{-j})\right\}

\displaystyle\geq-(3m-2)\|c\|_{\infty}.

Also notice that

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{j}^{*}(x_{j})

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\log\left\{\int\exp\left(\frac{\sum_{i\neq j}\hat{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})-c(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})}{\varepsilon}\right)d\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-j}(x_{-j})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\|c\|_{\infty}-\varepsilon\log\left\{\int\exp\left(\frac{\sum_{i\neq j}\hat{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})}{\varepsilon}\right)d\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-j}(x_{-j})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq(3m^{2}-5m+3)\,\|c\|_{\infty}.

∎

Lemma D.3
(Strong concavity of empirical dual objective).

The empirical dual objective
\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot)
is
\beta
-concave w.r.t the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}
on
\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{L}
with
\beta=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\exp\left(-\frac{L+\|c\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon}\right)
, i.e., for all
\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}\in\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{L}
,

\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f})-\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{g})\geq\left\langle\nabla\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}),(\mathbf{f}-\mathbf{g})\right\rangle_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}+\frac{\beta}{2}\|\mathbf{f}-\mathbf{g}\|_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}^{2}.

(44)

The proof is the same as the proof of Proposition
3.3
.
∎

The concentration of empirical potentials and joint density will be derived next. First, recall that we use
\bm{f}^{*}\in[\bm{f}^{*}]
to denote the optimal potential associated with
(\nu_{1},\dots,\nu_{m})
satisfying
\nu_{k}(f^{*}_{k})=0
for
k\in[m-1]

and
\hat{\bm{f}}^{*}\in[\hat{\bm{f}}^{*}]
to denote the optimal potential associated with
(\hat{\nu}^{N}_{1},\dots,\hat{\nu}^{N}_{m})
satisfying
\hat{\nu}^{N}_{k}(f^{*}_{k})=0
for
k\in[m-1]
. Next, we define

\bar{\mathbf{f}}^{*}=(\bar{f}^{*}_{1},\dots,\bar{f}^{*}_{m}):=\left(f^{*}_{1}-\hat{\nu}_{1}^{N}(f^{*}_{1}),\dots,f^{*}_{m-1}-\hat{\nu}_{m-1}^{N}(f^{*}_{m-1}),f^{*}_{m}+\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\hat{\nu}_{k}^{N}(f^{*}_{k})\right).

In view of the bounded dual potential proposition, we know that almost surely,
\bar{\mathbf{f}}^{*}\in\hat{\mathcal{S}}_{(2m+1)\|c\|_{\infty}}.
The following lemmas concerning
\bar{\mathbf{f}}^{*}
will be useful in the upcoming analysis.

Proposition D.4
.

For
\bm{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})
, we have

|\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})|\lesssim_{m,\varepsilon}\sum_{j=1}^{m}|\bar{f}_{j}^{*}(x_{j})-\hat{f}^{*}_{j}(x_{j})|.

(45)

By the Lipschitzness of
h(\cdot)=\exp(\cdot)
on a bounded domain, we have

\displaystyle|\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})|

\displaystyle=\left|\exp\left(\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{f}^{*}_{j}(x_{j})-c(\bm{x})}{\varepsilon}\right)-\exp\left(\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}f^{*}_{j}(x_{j})-c(\bm{x})}{\varepsilon}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\exp\left(\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{f}^{*}_{j}(x_{j})-c(\bm{x})}{\varepsilon}\right)-\exp\left(\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\bar{f}^{*}_{j}(x_{j})-c(\bm{x})}{\varepsilon}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{m,\varepsilon}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{f}^{*}_{j}(x_{j})-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\bar{f}^{*}_{j}(x_{j})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left|\hat{f}^{*}_{j}(x_{j})-\bar{f}^{*}_{j}(x_{j})\right|.

∎

Lemma D.5
(Bound on empirical potential functions).

\|\hat{\mathbf{f}}^{*}-\bar{\mathbf{f}}^{*}\|^{2}_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}\leq\varepsilon^{2}\exp\left(\frac{(4m+4)\|c\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon}\right)\|\nabla\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\|^{2}_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}.

Due to the strong concavity proved in Lemma
D.3
, we have

\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{\mathbf{f}}^{*})-\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\bar{\mathbf{f}}^{*})\geq\frac{1}{2\varepsilon}\exp\left({-\frac{(2m+2)\|c\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon}}\right)\|\hat{\mathbf{f}}^{*}-\bar{\mathbf{f}}^{*}\|^{2}_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}.

Also, the Polyak-Łojasiewicz inequality stated in Lemma
J.2
indicates that

\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{\mathbf{f}}^{*})-\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\bar{\mathbf{f}}^{*})\leq\frac{\varepsilon\exp(\frac{(2m+2)\|c\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon})}{2}\|\nabla\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\bar{\mathbf{f}}^{*})\|^{2}_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}=\frac{\varepsilon\exp(\frac{(2m+2)\|c\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon})}{2}\|\nabla\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\|^{2}_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}.

The observation above implies the lemma.
∎

Proposition D.6
(Concentration of empirical joint density of optimal coupling).

For
t>0
, with probability more than
1-2m(m-1)e^{-t}
,

\|p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})-\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}_{j}^{N})}\lesssim_{m,\varepsilon}\frac{t}{N}.

By Proposition
D.4
, it holds that

|p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})-\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})|\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\sum_{j=1}^{m}|\bar{f}_{j}^{*}(x_{j})-\hat{f}^{*}_{j}(x_{j})|.

Lemma
C.1
and Lemma
D.5
yield that with probability exceeding
1-2m(m-1)e^{-t}
,

\|\hat{\mathbf{f}}^{*}-\bar{\mathbf{f}}^{*}\|^{2}_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\|\nabla\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\|^{2}_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}\lesssim_{m,\varepsilon}\frac{t}{N}.

Thus, with probability at least
1-2m(m-1)e^{-t}
,

\displaystyle\|p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})-\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}_{j}^{N})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\varepsilon}(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}_{j}^{N})\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m}|\bar{f}_{j}^{*}(x_{j})-\hat{f}^{*}_{j}(x_{j})|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\|\hat{\mathbf{f}}^{*}-\bar{\mathbf{f}}^{*}\|^{2}_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}\lesssim_{m,\varepsilon}\frac{t}{N}.

∎

The lemmas above make us ready for the proof of theorem
4.3
.

Put
\bm{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})
. Note that

\displaystyle|(\pi_{\varepsilon}^{*}-\hat{\pi}^{N}_{\varepsilon})(g)|=

\displaystyle\left|\int g\,p^{*}_{\varepsilon}d\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{j}-\int g\,\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}d\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\int gp^{*}_{\varepsilon}d\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}-\int g\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}d\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\int g\,p^{*}_{\varepsilon}d\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{j}-\int g\,p^{*}_{\varepsilon}d\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\infty}\|p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})-\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})\|_{L^{1}(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}_{j}^{N})}+\left|\left(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}-\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{j}\right)(g\,p^{*}_{\varepsilon})\right|

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle(A)+(B).

(46)

By Proposition
D.6
, with probability at least
1-2m(m-1)e^{-t}
, the first term (A) above satisfies

\|p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})-\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})\|_{L^{1}(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}_{j}^{N})}\leq\|p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})-\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})\|_{L^{2}(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}_{j}^{N})}\lesssim_{m,\varepsilon}\sqrt{\frac{t}{N}}.

(47)

The bound for the second term (B) follows from Lemma
D.1
and the boundedness of the potentials from Proposition
2.2
. Indeed, with probability exceeding
1-2e^{-t}
,

\left|\left(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}-\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{j}\right)(g\,p^{*}_{\varepsilon})\right|\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\frac{2t}{N}}\|g\|_{\infty}.

∎

It is sufficient to show that

\displaystyle\|[\bm{f}^{*}]-[\hat{\bm{f}}^{*}]\|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}}\leq\|\bar{\bm{f}}^{*}-\hat{\bm{f}}^{*}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{N}}\right).

For that to hold, it is enough to establish

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{x}}\left\|\bar{\bm{f}}^{*}(\bm{x})-\hat{\bm{f}}^{*}(\bm{x})\right\|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{N}}\right),\quad\text{and}\quad\sup_{\bm{x}}\left\|\nabla\bar{\bm{f}}^{*}(\bm{x})-\nabla\hat{\bm{f}}^{*}(\bm{x})\right\|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{N}}\right).

In this proof, to shorten the notation, given a function

F:\underbrace{\mathbb{R}^{d}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbb{R}^{d}}_{m\text{ times}}\to\mathbb{R},

and
X_{1}^{(j)},\dots,X_{N}^{(j)}\overset{i.i.d.}{\sim}\nu_{j}

we define
F_{-i}(x_{i},\bm{X}_{\bm{l}_{(-i)}})
as

\displaystyle F\left(X_{\ell^{(1)}}^{(1)},\dots,X_{\ell^{(i-1)}}^{(i-1)},x_{i},X_{\ell^{(i+1)}}^{(i+1)},\dots,X_{\ell^{(m)}}^{(m)}\right),

where we denote
(X_{\ell^{(1)}}^{(1)},\dots,X_{\ell^{(i-1)}}^{(i-1)},x_{i},X_{\ell^{(i+1)}}^{(i+1)},\dots,X_{\ell^{(m)}}^{(m)})
as

(x_{i},\bm{X}_{\bm{l}_{(-i)}})
for
\bm{l}_{(-i)}=(\ell^{(1)},\dots,\ell^{(i-1)},\ell^{(i+1)},\dots,\ell^{(m)})
.

Step 1.
We first prove the first asymptotic equality. It is sufficient to show

\displaystyle\sup_{x_{i}\in[-1,1]^{d}}|\bar{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})-\hat{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{N}}\right).

Define
\tilde{f}^{*}_{i}(x_{i}):=-\varepsilon\log\left(\frac{1}{N^{m-1}}\sum_{\bm{l}_{(-i)}\in[N]^{m-1}}e^{\frac{\left(\oplus_{j\neq i}\bar{f}_{j}^{*}-c\right)_{-i}\left(x_{i},\bm{X}_{\bm{l}_{-i}}\right)}{\varepsilon}}\right).
On the one hand,

\displaystyle|\bar{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})

\displaystyle-\tilde{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})|

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\left|\log\left(\frac{1}{N^{m-1}}\sum_{\bm{l}_{(-i)}\in[N]^{m-1}}e^{\frac{\left(\oplus_{j\neq i}\bar{f}_{j}^{*}-c\right)_{-i}\left(x_{i},\bm{X}_{\bm{l}_{-i}}\right)}{\varepsilon}}\right)-\log\int e^{\frac{\oplus_{j\neq i}\bar{f}_{j}^{*}(x_{j})-c(x_{i},x_{-i})}{\varepsilon}}d\nu_{-i}(\bm{x})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\frac{1}{N^{m-1}}\sum_{\bm{l}_{(-i)}\in[N]^{m-1}}e^{\frac{\left(\oplus_{j\neq i}\bar{f}_{j}^{*}-c\right)_{-i}\left(x_{i},\bm{X}_{\bm{l}_{-i}}\right)}{\varepsilon}}-\int e^{\frac{\oplus_{j\neq i}\bar{f}_{j}^{*}(x_{j})-c(x_{i},x_{-i})}{\varepsilon}}d\nu_{-i}(\bm{x})\right|.

By Lemma
G.6
, we know that

\displaystyle\sup_{x_{i}}|\bar{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})

\displaystyle-\tilde{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{N}}\right).

On the other hand,

\displaystyle|

\displaystyle\tilde{f}^{*}_{i}(x_{i})-\hat{f}^{*}_{i}(x_{i})|

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\left|\log\left(\frac{1}{N^{m-1}}\sum_{\bm{l}_{(-i)}\in[N]^{m-1}}e^{\frac{\left(\oplus_{j\neq i}\bar{f}_{j}^{*}-c\right)_{-i}\left(x_{i},\bm{X}_{\bm{l}_{-i}}\right)}{\varepsilon}}\right)-\log\left(\frac{1}{N^{m-1}}\sum_{\bm{l}_{(-i)}\in[N]^{m-1}}e^{\frac{\left(\oplus_{j\neq i}\hat{f}_{j}^{*}-c\right)_{-i}\left(x_{i},\bm{X}_{\bm{l}_{-i}}\right)}{\varepsilon}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{m-1}}\sum_{\bm{l}_{(-i)}\in[N]^{m-1}}\left|e^{\frac{\left(\oplus_{j\neq i}\bar{f}_{j}^{*}-c\right)_{-i}\left(x_{i},\bm{X}_{\bm{l}_{-i}}\right)}{\varepsilon}}-e^{\frac{\left(\oplus_{j\neq i}\hat{f}_{j}^{*}-c\right)_{-i}\left(x_{i},\bm{X}_{\bm{l}_{-i}}\right)}{\varepsilon}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\neq i\\
j\in[m]\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}|\hat{f}^{N}_{j}-\bar{f}^{*}_{j}|(X_{\ell}^{(j)}).

Based on Lemma
C.1
and Lemma
D.5
, we know that

\sup_{x_{i}}\sum_{j\neq i}\|\bar{f}^{*}_{j}-\hat{f}^{*}_{j}\|_{L^{1}(\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j})}\lesssim\|\nabla\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\|_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(N^{-1/2}).

Step 2.
Now, we prove the second asymptotic equality. Based on the topology we have, it is equivalent to show

\displaystyle\sup_{x_{i}\in[-1,1]^{d}}\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial_{x_{i,k}}}\left(\bar{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})-\hat{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})\right)\right|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{N}}\right).

Taking derivative, and we have via multimarginal Schrödinger system that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial_{x_{i,k}}}\bar{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\partial}{\partial_{x_{i,k}}}c(\bm{x})p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})d\nu_{-i}(\bm{x}),

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial_{x_{i,k}}}\hat{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\partial}{\partial_{x_{i,k}}}c(\bm{x})\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})d\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-i}(\bm{x}).

Define
S(x_{i}):=\int\frac{\partial}{\partial_{x_{i,k}}}c(\bm{x})p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})d\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-i}(\bm{x})
and triangle inequality gives

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial_{x_{i,k}}}\hat{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})-\frac{\partial}{\partial_{x_{i,k}}}\bar{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})\right|\leq\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial_{x_{i,k}}}\hat{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})-S(x_{i})\right|+\left|S(x_{i})-\frac{\partial}{\partial_{x_{i,k}}}\bar{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})\right|.

By Lemma
G.6
,

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\sup_{x_{i}\in[-1,1]^{d}}\left|S(x_{i})-\frac{\partial}{\partial_{x_{i,k}}}\bar{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})\right|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{N}}\right).

The other term could be bounded as follows.

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial_{x_{i,k}}}\hat{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})-S(x_{i})\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int\frac{\partial}{\partial_{x_{i,k}}}c(\bm{x})p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})d\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-i}(\bm{x})-\int\frac{\partial}{\partial_{x_{i,k}}}c(\bm{x})\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})d\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-i}(\bm{x})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int\left|p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})-\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})\right|d\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-i}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{m,\varepsilon}|\bar{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})-\widehat{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})|+\sum_{j\neq i}\|\bar{f}_{j}^{*}-\widehat{f}_{j}^{*}\|_{L^{1}(\widehat{\nu}_{j}^{N})}.

The first term on the right hand side is bounded in Step 1. For the second term on the right hand side, based on Lemma
C.1
and Lemma
D.5
, we know that

\displaystyle\sup_{x_{i}}\sum_{j\neq i}\|\bar{f}_{j}^{*}-\hat{f}_{j}^{*}\|_{L^{1}(\widehat{\nu}_{j}^{N})}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{m-1}\Bigl(\sum_{j\neq i}\|\bar{f}_{j}^{*}-\hat{f}_{j}^{*}\|_{L^{2}(\widehat{\nu}_{j}^{N})}^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{m,\varepsilon}\|\nabla\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\|_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(N^{-1/2}).

∎

Lemma D.7
(Uniform bound on empirical joint density of optimal coupling).

For a given cost function
c\in\mathcal{C}^{2}
,

\sup_{x_{i}\in[-1,1]^{d}}\int\left|(\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}-p^{*}_{\varepsilon})(\bm{x})\right|^{2}d\hat{\nu}_{-i}^{N}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{d\log N}{N}\right).

By Proposition
D.4
, we have

|\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})|^{2}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}m\sum_{j=1}^{m}|\bar{f}_{j}^{*}(x_{j})-\hat{f}^{*}_{j}(x_{j})|^{2}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\sup_{x_{i}\in[-1,1]^{d}}\int\left|(\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}-p^{*}_{\varepsilon})(\bm{x})\right|^{2}d\hat{\nu}_{-i}^{N}\lesssim_{m,\varepsilon}\sup_{x_{i}\in[-1,1]^{d}}|\bar{f}_{i}^{*}(x_{i})-\hat{f}^{*}_{i}(x_{i})|^{2}+\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell^{(j)}=1}^{N}\left|\bar{f}_{j}^{*}(X^{(j)}_{\ell^{(j)}})-\hat{f}^{*}_{j}(X^{(j)}_{\ell^{(j)}})\right|^{2}.

The first term on the right hand side is dealt with in Lemma
4.8
.
The second term on the right hand side is bounded by Lemma
C.1
and Lemma
D.5
.
∎

In this proof, we shall denote
C>0
as a generic constant depending only on
m,\varepsilon,\beta
and
d
, whose value may vary from line to line. From (
46
) in the proof of Theorem
4.3
, we know that for every
g\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}
,
|(\pi_{\varepsilon}^{*}-\hat{\pi}^{N}_{\varepsilon})(g)|
can be bounded as following

\displaystyle|(\pi_{\varepsilon}^{*}-\hat{\pi}^{N}_{\varepsilon})(g)|\leq\|g\|_{\infty}\|p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})-\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})\|_{L^{1}(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}_{j}^{N})}+\left|\left(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}-\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{j}\right)(g\,p^{*}_{\varepsilon})\right|

As a result,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{g\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}}|(\pi_{\varepsilon}^{*}-\hat{\pi}^{N}_{\varepsilon})(g)|\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{g\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}}\left[\|g\|_{\infty}\|p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})-\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})\|_{L^{1}(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}_{j}^{N})}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\sup_{g\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}}\left|\left(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}-\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{j}\right)(gp^{*}_{\varepsilon})\right|

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle(\text{I})+(\text{II}).

Note that we have proved that for any
t>0
, (
47
) holds with probability at least
1-2m(m-1)e^{-t}
.
Integrating this tail probability bound, we have the following expectation bound for term
(\text{I})

\mathbb{E}\sup_{g\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}}\left[\|g\|_{\infty}\|p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})-\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})\|_{L^{1}(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}_{j}^{N})}\right]\lesssim_{m,\varepsilon}L/\sqrt{N}.

(48)

Next, we are going to control term
(\text{II})
. By the product telescoping identity,

\displaystyle\biggl(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{m}\widehat{\nu}_{i}^{N}-\bigotimes_{i=1}^{m}\nu_{i}\biggr)(gp_{\varepsilon}^{*})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int\Bigl(\widehat{\nu}_{j}^{N}-\nu_{j}\Bigr)\Bigl(p_{\varepsilon}^{*}(\cdot,x_{-j})g(\cdot,x_{-j})\Bigr)\,d\biggl[\Bigl(\bigotimes_{k<j}\widehat{\nu}_{k}^{N}\Bigr)\otimes\Bigl(\bigotimes_{k>j}\nu_{k}\Bigr)\biggr](x_{-j}).

For every fixed
x_{-j}
, restriction to the
j
-th coordinate block gives

\|g(\cdot,x_{-j})\|_{\beta}\leq\|g\|_{\beta}\leq L,

so
g(\cdot,x_{-j})\in\mathcal{H}([-1,1]^{d};\beta,L)
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\sup_{g\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}}\Biggl|\biggl(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{m}\widehat{\nu}_{i}^{N}-\bigotimes_{i=1}^{m}\nu_{i}\biggl)(gp_{\varepsilon}^{*})\Biggl|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}([-1,1]^{d};\beta,L)}\left|(\widehat{\nu}_{j}^{N}-\nu_{j})\left(p_{\varepsilon}^{*}(\cdot,x_{-j})h\right)\right|d\biggl[\Bigl(\bigotimes_{k<j}\widehat{\nu}_{k}^{N}\Bigr)\otimes\Bigl(\bigotimes_{k>j}\nu_{k}\Bigr)\biggr](x_{-j}).

For fixed
x_{-j}
, the function class

\left\{p_{\varepsilon}^{*}(\cdot,x_{-j})h:h\in\mathcal{H}([-1,1]^{d};\beta,L)\right\}

has envelope
L\|p_{\varepsilon}^{*}\|_{\infty}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\log N\left(\left\{p_{\varepsilon}^{*}(\cdot,x_{-j})h:h\in\mathcal{H}([-1,1]^{d};\beta,L)\right\},\eta,\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\log N\left(\mathcal{H}([-1,1]^{d};\beta,L),\frac{\eta}{\|p_{\varepsilon}^{*}\|_{\infty}},\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)\lesssim_{\beta,d}\left(\frac{L}{\eta}\right)^{d/\beta}.

The same chaining argument
as in the proof of Theorem 5.5, equations (
51
)–(
59
) yields

\sup_{x_{-j}}\mathbb{E}_{j}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}([-1,1]^{d};\beta,L)}\left|(\widehat{\nu}_{j}^{N}-\nu_{j})\left(p_{\varepsilon}^{*}(\cdot,x_{-j})h\right)\right|\lesssim\begin{cases}LN^{-1/2},&d<2\beta,\\
LN^{-1/2}\log(eN),&d=2\beta,\\
LN^{-\beta/d},&d>2\beta,\end{cases}

where
\mathbb{E}_{j}
denotes expectation over the
j
-th marginal sample. Taking expectations
and summing over
j\in[m]
, we obtain

(\mathrm{II})\lesssim\begin{cases}LN^{-1/2},&d<2\beta,\\
LN^{-1/2}\log N,&d=2\beta,\\
LN^{-\beta/d},&d>2\beta.\end{cases}

Here the implicit constants may depend on

m,\varepsilon,\|c\|_{\infty},\beta,d
, but not on
N
or
L
.
Combining this estimate with (
48
) completes the proof of (
17
). The proof of
W_{p}
bound for
p=1,2
follows exactly the same as that in the proof of Theorem
5.5
.

∎

In this proof, we shall denote
C>0
as a generic constant depending only on
m,\varepsilon,\beta
and
d
, whose value may vary from line to line. From (
46
) in the proof of Theorem
4.3
, we know that for every
h\in\mathcal{H}
,
|(\bar{\nu}_{\varepsilon}-\hat{\nu}^{N}_{\varepsilon})(h)|
can be bounded as following

\displaystyle|(\bar{\nu}_{\varepsilon}-\hat{\nu}^{N}_{\varepsilon})(h)|

\displaystyle\leq\|h\|_{\infty}\|p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})-\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})\|_{L^{1}(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}_{j}^{N})}+\left|\left(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}-\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{j}\right)(p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(h\circ T_{\alpha}))\right|.

As a result,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}|(\bar{\nu}_{\varepsilon}-\hat{\nu}^{N}_{\varepsilon})(h)|\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\left[\|h\|_{\infty}\|p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})-\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})\|_{L^{1}(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}_{j}^{N})}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\left|\left(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}-\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{j}\right)(p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(h\circ T_{\alpha}))\right|

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle(A)+(B).

(49)

Note that we have proved that for any
t>0
, (
47
) holds with probability at least
1-2m(m-1)e^{-t}
.
Integrating this tail probability bound, we have the following expectation bound for term
(A)

\mathbb{E}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\left[\|h\|_{\infty}\|p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})-\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})\|_{L^{1}(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}_{j}^{N})}\right]\lesssim_{m,\varepsilon}L/\sqrt{N}.

(50)

Next, we are going to control term
(B)
. Let
X_{h}:=\sqrt{N}\left(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}-\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{j}\right)(p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(h\circ T_{\alpha}))
be a mean-zero process indexed by
h\in\mathcal{H}
. By Lemma
D.1
, we know that with probability more than
1-2e^{-t}
,

\left|X_{h}-X_{\tilde{h}}\right|\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\|h-\tilde{h}\|_{\infty}\sqrt{t}.

(51)

Thus,
X_{h}
satisfies the sub-Gaussian condition in Lemma
J.4
. For any given
h\in\mathcal{H}
, it is obvious from (
51
) that
\mathbb{E}|X_{h}|\lesssim_{m,\varepsilon}L
. From Theorem 2.7.1 in
[
89
]
, we know

\log N(\mathcal{H},\eta,\|\cdot\|_{\infty})\lesssim_{\beta,d}\Big({\frac{L}{\eta}}\Big)^{d/\beta}.

(52)

Case 1:
d<2\beta
. By (
52
), it is clear that
\int_{0}^{2L}\sqrt{\log N\left(\mathcal{H},\varepsilon,\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)}d\varepsilon<\infty
under this setting. Starting from the increment condition (
51
), one can obtain, via the usual Dudley’s bound (see, for example, Lemma
J.4
with
\delta=0
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}|\left(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}-\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{j}\right)(p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(h\circ T_{\alpha}))|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{m,\varepsilon}\ N^{-1/2}\int_{0}^{2L}\sqrt{\log N\left(\mathcal{H},\varepsilon,\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)}d\varepsilon

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\beta,d}L/\sqrt{N}.

(53)

Combining (
49
), (
50
) and (
53
), we see that there is some constant
C=C(m,\varepsilon,\beta,d)>0
such that

\mathbb{E}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}|(\bar{\nu}_{\varepsilon}-\hat{\nu}^{N}_{\varepsilon})(h)|\leq CLN^{-1/2}.

(54)

Case 2:
d>2\beta
.
We have
D=:\sup_{h,\tilde{h}\in\mathcal{H}}\|h-\tilde{h}\|_{\infty}\leq 2L
. For any
\delta\in[0,D]
, notice that

\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{\{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}:\;\|\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq\delta\}}(X_{\gamma}-X_{\gamma^{\prime}})\Big]\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{N}\delta,

(55)

and

\int_{\delta/4}^{D}\sqrt{\log N(\mathcal{H},\varepsilon,\|\cdot\|_{\infty})}d\varepsilon\lesssim_{\beta,d}L^{\frac{d}{2\beta}}\int_{\delta/4}^{2L}\varepsilon^{-\frac{d}{2\beta}}\,d\varepsilon\lesssim_{\beta,d}L^{\frac{d}{2\beta}}\delta^{-\frac{d-2\beta}{2\beta}}.

(56)

By virtue of (
55
) and (
56
), applying Lemma
J.4
, for any
\delta\in[0,D]
, it holds true that

\mathbb{E}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\left|\sqrt{N}\left(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}-\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{j}\right)(p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(h\circ T_{\alpha}))\right|\leq C[L+\sqrt{N}\delta+L^{\frac{d}{2\beta}}\delta^{-\frac{d-2\beta}{2\beta}}].

Optimize over
\delta\in[0,D]
, and we know that for
\delta\asymp LN^{-\beta/d}
, we have

\mathbb{E}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\left|\left(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}-\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{j}\right)(p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(h\circ T_{\alpha}))\right|\leq CLN^{-\beta/d},

(57)

for some constant
C>0
. Combining (
49
), (
50
) and (
57
), the result under Case 2 is proved.

Case 3:
d=2\beta
. The argument under this setting is similar to that for the previous case. Inequalities
(\ref{nonp1})
and
\mathbb{E}|X_{h}|\lesssim_{m,\varepsilon}L

still hold. Additionally, observe that for
d=2\beta
,

\int_{\delta/4}^{D}\sqrt{\log N(\mathcal{H},\varepsilon,\|\cdot\|_{\infty})}d\varepsilon\lesssim_{\beta,d}L\int_{\delta/4}^{2L}\varepsilon^{-1}d\varepsilon=L\log(8L)+L\log(1/\delta).

(58)

Due to (
55
), (
58
), and Lemma
J.4
, we find that for some constant
C>0
,

\mathbb{E}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\left|\sqrt{N}\left(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}-\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{j}\right)(p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(h\circ T_{\alpha}))\right|\leq C[L\log(8eL)+\sqrt{N}\delta+L\log(1/\delta)].

Taking
\delta\asymp LN^{-1/2}\log{N}
, the above bound becomes

\mathbb{E}\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\left|\left(\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}-\otimes_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{j}\right)(p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(h\circ T_{\alpha}))\right|\leq CLN^{-1/2}\log{N}.

(59)

The combination of (
49
), (
50
) and (
59
) leads to the conclusion in (
25
).

Finally, we prove (
26
). For the
W_{1}
bound, invoking the well-known Kantorovich duality
[
91
]
,

W_{1}(\bar{\nu}_{\varepsilon},\hat{\nu}^{N}_{\varepsilon})=\sup\left\{\Bigl|\int fd\bar{\nu}_{\varepsilon}-\int fd\hat{\nu}^{N}_{\varepsilon}\Bigr|:f\in\mathcal{H}([-1,1]^{d};1,1)\right\},

where we recall that
\mathcal{H}([-1,1]^{d};1,1)
is the class of Lipschitz continuous functions with constant one. This observation concludes the proof combined with the covering bound
\log N(\mathcal{H}([-1,1]^{d};1,1),\eta,\|\cdot\|_{\infty})\lesssim_{d}\eta^{-d}
from Example 5.10 in
[
92
]
. As for
W_{2}
bound in (
26
), by arguments in Lemma 3 of
[
31
]
,

\displaystyle W^{2}_{2}(\bar{\nu}_{\varepsilon},\hat{\nu}^{N}_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\mu}|W^{2}_{2}(\bar{\nu}_{\varepsilon},\mu)-W^{2}_{2}(\hat{\nu}^{N}_{\varepsilon},\mu)|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup\left\{\Bigl|\int fd\bar{\nu}_{\varepsilon}-\int fd\hat{\nu}^{N}_{\varepsilon}\Bigr|:f\in\mathcal{H}([-1,1]^{d};1,1)\cap\text{Conv}([-1,1]^{d})\right\}+\left|\int\|\cdot\|^{2}d(\bar{\nu}_{\varepsilon}-\hat{\nu}^{N}_{\varepsilon})\right|,

with
\text{Conv}([-1,1]^{d})
the collection of convex functions
f:[-1,1]^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
. From
[
14
]
, we have
\log N(\mathcal{H}([-1,1]^{d};1,1)\cap\text{Conv}([-1,1]^{d}),\eta,\|\cdot\|_{\infty})\lesssim_{d}\eta^{-d/2}
for the first term above. The second term of the preceding display has already been well-controlled by Theorem
4.3
. Finally, (
26
) follows from (
25
) with
\beta=p
for
p=1
and
p=2
.

∎

E
Sample complexity and weak limit of the cost functional

The proof of sample complexity (Theorem
4.1
) and that of weak limit (Theorem
4.5
) of the cost functional are similar. We therefore report them together in this section.

By the strong duality between (
12
) and (
13
), we have

\mathbb{E}\left[S_{\varepsilon}(\nu_{1},\dots,\nu_{m})-S_{\varepsilon}(\hat{\nu}^{N}_{1},\dots,\hat{\nu}^{N}_{m})\right]^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})-\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{\mathbf{f}}^{*})\right]^{2}.

Decomposing

\Phi_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})-\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{\mathbf{f}}^{*})=\Phi_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})-\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})+\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})-\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{\mathbf{f}}^{*}),

we may bound

\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})-\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{\mathbf{f}}^{*})\right|^{2}\leq 2\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})-\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\right|^{2}+2\left|\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})-\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{\mathbf{f}}^{*})\right|^{2}=:(\text{I})+(\text{II}).

(60)

Term (I)
. Plugging into the definition of population and empirical dual objective functions in (
5
) and (
13
), we have

\begin{gathered}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Phi_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})-\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\Big]^{2}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=1}^{m}(v_{j}-\hat{v}^{N}_{j})(f_{j}^{*})+\varepsilon(\otimes_{k=1}^{m}\hat{v}^{N}_{k}-\otimes_{k=1}^{m}v_{k})\exp\Big(\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{m}f^{*}_{k}-c}{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big]^{2}\\
\leq 2\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=1}^{m}(v_{j}-\hat{v}^{N}_{j})(f_{j}^{*})\Big]^{2}+2\varepsilon^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[(\otimes_{k=1}^{m}\hat{v}^{N}_{k}-\otimes_{k=1}^{m}v_{k})\exp\Big(\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{m}f^{*}_{k}-c}{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big]^{2}.\end{gathered}

(61)

For the first term on the RHS of (
61
), we use the sample independence to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=1}^{m}(\nu_{j}-\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j})(f_{j}^{*})\Big]^{2}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\Big(f^{*}_{j}(X^{(j)}_{k})-\nu_{j}(f^{*}_{j})\Big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{1\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq m}\sum_{1\leq k,k^{\prime}\leq N}\left(f^{*}_{j}(X^{(j)}_{k})-\nu_{j}(f^{*}_{j})\right)\left(f^{*}_{j^{\prime}}(X^{(j^{\prime})}_{k^{\prime}})-\nu_{j^{\prime}}(f^{*}_{j^{\prime}})\right)\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\Big[\left(f^{*}_{j}(X_{1}^{(j)})-\nu_{j}(f^{*}_{j})\right)^{2}\Big]=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\text{Var}_{\nu_{j}}(f^{*}_{j})\leq\frac{m\|c\|_{\infty}^{2}}{N},

(62)

where the last inequality follows from the pointwise boundedness of the dual potentials in Proposition
2.2
. For the second term on the RHS of (
61
), we use the marginal feasibility constrain (
7
) for the multimarginal Schrödinger system to deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[(\otimes_{k=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{k}-\otimes_{k=1}^{m}\nu_{k})\exp\Big(\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{m}f^{*}_{k}-c}{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big]^{2}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\left(\otimes_{k=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{k}\right)\Big(1-\exp\Big(\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{m}f^{*}_{k}-c}{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2m}}\mathbb{E}\Big\{\sum_{1\leq k^{(1)},\dots,k^{(m)}\leq N}\Big[1-\exp\Big({\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{m}f^{*}_{i}(X^{(i)}_{k^{(i)}})-c(X^{(1)}_{k^{(1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{k^{(m)}})}{\varepsilon}}\Big)\Big]\Big\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2m}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k^{(1)},\dots,k^{(m)}\leq N\\
1\leq k^{\prime(1)},\dots,k^{\prime(m)}\leq N\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(X^{(1)}_{k^{(1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{k^{(m)}})\right)\left(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(X^{(1)}_{k^{\prime(1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{k^{\prime(m)}})\right)\right],

where
\mathbb{E}[(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(X^{(1)}_{k^{(1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{k^{(m)}}))(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(X^{(1)}_{k^{\prime(1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{k^{\prime(m)}}))]=0
,
if one of the following is true:

(i)

k^{(j)}\neq k^{\prime(j)}
, for every
j\in[m]
, which leverages the independence;

(ii)

k^{(j_{0})}=k^{\prime(j_{0})}
for some
j_{0}\in[m]
and
k^{(j)}\neq k^{\prime(j)}
, for every
j\in[m]\backslash\{j_{0}\}
, due to the marginal feasibility condition (
7
) of the multimarginal Schrödinger system.

Note that there are
(N(N-1))^{m}
many terms in Case (i) and
mN(N(N-1))^{(m-1)}
many terms in Case (ii). Thus we have

\displaystyle(\ref{cross1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{N^{2m}-N^{m}(N-1)^{m}-mN^{m}(N-1)^{m-1}}{N^{2m}}\exp\left(\frac{(4m+4)\|c\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\frac{(4m+4)\|c\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon}\right)\left(1-(1-\frac{1}{N})^{m-1}\right)\leq\exp\left(\frac{(4m+4)\|c\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon}\right)\frac{m}{N}.

(64)

Combining (
61
), (
62
) and (
64
), we establish

\mathbb{E}(\text{I})\leq\frac{4m\|c\|_{\infty}^{2}}{N}+\frac{4m\varepsilon^{2}}{N}\exp\left(\frac{(4m+4)\|c\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon}\right).

(65)

Term (II)
. Since
\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot)
is strongly concave (cf. Lemma
D.3
), it follows from the Polyak-Łojasiewicz (PL) inequality in Lemma
J.2
that

\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})-\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{\mathbf{f}}^{*})\right]^{2}\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{4}\exp{\left(\frac{(4m+4)\|c\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon}\right)}\mathbb{E}\|\nabla\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\|_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}^{4}.

(66)

Using (
43
), the empirical dual version of Lemma
3.2
, we find that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\nabla\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\|_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int\Big[\int(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon})d\otimes_{i\neq j}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{i}\Big]^{2}d\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2m-1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq m\\
1\leq k^{(j)}\leq N\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{1\leq\ell^{(1)},\dots,\ell^{(j-1)},\ell^{(j+1)},\dots,\ell^{(m)}\leq N}\left(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(X^{(1)}_{\ell^{(1)}},\dots,X^{(j-1)}_{\ell^{(j-1)}},X^{(j)}_{k^{(j)}},X^{(j+1)}_{\ell^{(j+1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{\ell^{(m)}})\right)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2m-1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq m\\
1\leq k^{(j)}\leq N\\
1\leq\ell^{(1)},\dots,\ell^{(j-1)},\ell^{(j+1)},\dots,\ell^{(m)}\leq N\\
1\leq\ell^{\prime(1)},\dots,\ell^{\prime(j-1)},\ell^{\prime(j+1)},\dots,\ell^{\prime(m)}\leq N\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(X^{(1)}_{\ell^{(1)}},\dots,X^{(j-1)}_{\ell^{(j-1)}},X^{(j)}_{k^{(j)}},X^{(j+1)}_{\ell^{(j+1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{\ell^{(m)}})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\times\left.\left(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(X^{(1)}_{\ell^{\prime(1)}},\dots,X^{(j-1)}_{\ell^{\prime(j-1)}},X^{(j)}_{k^{(j)}},X^{(j+1)}_{\ell^{\prime(j+1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{\ell^{\prime(m)}})\right)\right],

(67)

where the summand in the last expression equals to

\displaystyle\text{Cov}\left[p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(X^{(1)}_{\ell^{(1)}},\dots,X^{(j-1)}_{\ell^{(j-1)}},X^{(j)}_{k^{(j)}},X^{(j+1)}_{\ell^{(j+1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{\ell^{(m)}}),\,p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(X^{(1)}_{\ell^{\prime(1)}},\dots,X^{(j-1)}_{\ell^{\prime(j-1)}},X^{(j)}_{k^{(j)}},X^{(j+1)}_{\ell^{\prime(j+1)}},\cdots,X^{(m)}_{\ell^{\prime(m)}})\right]

\displaystyle=0,\qquad\text{whenever}

(iii)

\ell^{(i)}\neq\ell^{\prime(i)}
, for every
i\neq j
, once again due to the marginal feasibility constraints (
7
) of the multimarginal Schrödinger system.

Note that there are
mN\left(N(N-1)\right)^{(m-1)}
many terms in case (iii), so we can bound

\displaystyle(\ref{cross2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{mN^{2m-1}-mN^{m}(N-1)^{m-1}}{N^{2m-1}}\exp\Big(\frac{(2m+2)\|c\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon}\Big)

\displaystyle=m\exp\Big(\frac{(2m+2)\|c\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big(1-(1-\frac{1}{N})^{(m-1)}\Big)\leq\exp\Big(\frac{(2m+2)\|c\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon}\Big)\frac{m^{2}}{N}.

(68)

At the same time, since the dual potentials are bounded as Proposition
2.2
, we have

\|\nabla\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\|_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int\Big(\int(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon})d\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-j}\Big)^{2}d\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}\leq m\exp\Big(\frac{(4m+4)\|c\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon}\Big).

(69)

Combining (
66
), (
67
), (
68
) and (
69
), we get

\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})-\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{\mathbf{f}}^{*})\right]^{2}\leq\frac{m^{3}\varepsilon^{2}}{4N}\exp{\left(\frac{(10m+10)\|c\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon}\right)}.

(70)

Finally, putting all (
60
), (
65
) and (
70
) pieces together, we obtain the desired bound

\mathbb{E}\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})-\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{\mathbf{f}}^{*})\right|^{2}\leq\frac{C}{N},

(71)

where
C=C({m,\varepsilon,\|c\|_{\infty}})>0
is a constant depending only on
m,\varepsilon,\|c\|_{\infty}
.
∎

Decomposing

\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{\mathbf{f}}^{*})-\Phi_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})=\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})-\Phi_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})+\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{\mathbf{f}}^{*})-\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*}):=(\text{I})+(\text{II}).

Term (I)

To shorten the notation, we write

\displaystyle U_{N}

\displaystyle:=\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})-\Phi_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left(f^{*}_{j}(X^{(j)}_{k})-\int f^{*}_{j}d\nu_{j}\right)-\frac{\varepsilon}{N^{m}}\sum_{1\leq k^{(1)},\dots,k^{(m)}\leq N}\Big(p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(X^{(1)}_{k^{(1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{k^{(m)}})-1\Big).

Now, we define

U_{N,\,\text{proj}}:=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left(f^{*}_{j}(X^{(k)}_{j})-\int f^{*}_{j}d\nu_{j}\right).

It is easily observed that
\text{Var}(U_{N,\,\text{proj}})=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\text{Var}_{X_{j}\sim\nu_{j}}(f^{*}_{j}(X_{j}))
and hence one easily obtains that

\sqrt{N}\,U_{N,\,\text{proj}}\overset{w}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\sum_{j=1}^{m}\text{Var}_{X_{j}\sim\nu_{j}}(f^{*}_{j}(X_{j}))\right).

Denote
R_{N}=U_{N}-U_{N,\,\text{proj}}=\frac{-\varepsilon}{N^{m}}\sum_{k^{(1)},\dots,k^{(m)}}\Big(p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(X^{(1)}_{k^{(1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{k^{(m)}})-1\Big)
and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality gives

\displaystyle\left|\text{Var}(U_{N})-(\text{Var}(U_{N,\,\text{proj}})+\text{Var}(R_{N}))\right|\leq 2\sqrt{\text{Var}(U_{N,\,\text{proj}})\text{Var}(R_{N})}.

The marginal feasibility constrain (
7
) for the multimarginal Schrödinger system implies that

\displaystyle\text{Var}(R_{N})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}R^{2}_{N}=\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{N^{2m}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{1\leq k^{(1)},\dots,k^{(m)}\leq N}\Big(p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(X^{(1)}_{k^{(1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{k^{(m)}})-1\Big)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{N^{2m}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k^{(1)},\dots,k^{(m)}\leq N\\
1\leq k^{\prime(1)},\dots,k^{\prime(m)}\leq N\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(X^{(1)}_{k^{(1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{k^{(m)}})\right)\left(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(X^{(1)}_{k^{\prime(1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{k^{\prime(m)}})\right)\right],

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{N^{2m}}\sum_{r=0}^{m}\sum_{|\mathbf{S}|=r}\mathbb{E}\left(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{S}},\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{S}^{c}})\right)\left(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{S}},\mathbf{X}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{S}^{c}})\right).

Here, to lighten the notation, we use
\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{S}}
to indicate the same elements between
(X^{(1)}_{k^{(1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{k^{(m)}})
and
(X^{(1)}_{k^{\prime(1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{k^{\prime(m)}})
.
\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{S}^{c}}
(resp.
\mathbf{X}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{S}^{c}}
) denotes the remaining elements in

(X^{(1)}_{k^{(1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{k^{(m)}})
(resp.
(X^{(1)}_{k^{\prime(1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{k^{\prime(m)}})
). Note that

\mathbb{E}\left(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{S}},\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{S}^{c}})\right)\left(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{S}},\mathbf{X}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{S}^{c}})\right)=0,

if one of the following is true:

(i)

|\mathbf{S}|=0
, namely,
k^{(j)}\neq k^{\prime(j)}
, for every
j\in[m]
, which leverages the independence;

(ii)

|\mathbf{S}|=1
, namely,
k^{(j_{0})}=k^{\prime(j_{0})}
for some
j_{0}\in[m]
and
k^{(j)}\neq k^{\prime(j)}
, for every
j\in[m]\backslash\{j_{0}\}
, due to the marginal feasibility condition (
7
) of the multimarginal Schrödinger system.

As a consequence,

\displaystyle\text{Var}(R_{N})

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{N^{2m}}\sum_{r=2}^{m}\sum_{|\mathbf{S}|=r}\mathbb{E}\left(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{S}},\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{S}^{c}})\right)\left(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{S}},\mathbf{X}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{S}^{c}})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{N^{2m}}\sum_{r=2}^{m}\binom{m}{r}N^{r}(N(N-1))^{(m-r)}e^{\frac{(2m+2)\|c\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon}}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\sum_{r=2}^{m}\frac{1}{N^{r}}\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{2}}.

(72)

So we have
\frac{\text{Var}(U_{N})}{\text{Var}(U_{N,\,\text{proj}})}\to 1
as
N\to\infty
in combination with (
E
) and thus

\frac{U_{N}}{\sqrt{\text{Var}(U_{N})}}-\frac{U_{N,\,\text{proj}}}{\sqrt{\text{Var}(U_{N,\,\text{proj}}})}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}0.

Slutsky’s theorem yields

\sqrt{N}\,U_{N}\overset{w}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\sum_{j=1}^{m}\text{Var}_{X_{j}\sim\nu_{j}}(f^{*}_{j}(X_{j}))\right).

(73)

Term (II)

Since
\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\cdot)
is strongly concave (cf. Lemma
D.3
), it follows from the Polyak-Łojasiewicz (PL) inequality in Lemma
J.2
that

0\leq\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{\mathbf{f}}^{*})-\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\exp\left(\frac{(2m+2)\|c\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon}\right)\|\nabla\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\|_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}^{2}.

(74)

Using (
43
), the empirical dual version of Lemma
3.2
, we find that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\nabla\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\|_{\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{m}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int\Big[\int(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon})d\otimes_{i\neq j}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{i}\Big]^{2}d\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2m-1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq m\\
1\leq k^{(j)}\leq N\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{1\leq\ell^{(1)},\dots,\ell^{(j-1)},\ell^{(j+1)},\dots,\ell^{(m)}\leq N}\left(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(X^{(1)}_{\ell^{(1)}},\dots,X^{(j-1)}_{\ell^{(j-1)}},X^{(j)}_{k^{(j)}},X^{(j+1)}_{\ell^{(j+1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{\ell^{(m)}})\right)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2m-1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq m\\
1\leq k^{(j)}\leq N\\
1\leq\ell^{(1)},\dots,\ell^{(j-1)},\ell^{(j+1)},\dots,\ell^{(m)}\leq N\\
1\leq\ell^{\prime(1)},\dots,\ell^{\prime(j-1)},\ell^{\prime(j+1)},\dots,\ell^{\prime(m)}\leq N\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(X^{(1)}_{\ell^{(1)}},\dots,X^{(j-1)}_{\ell^{(j-1)}},X^{(j)}_{k^{(j)}},X^{(j+1)}_{\ell^{(j+1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{\ell^{(m)}})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\times\left.\left(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(X^{(1)}_{\ell^{\prime(1)}},\dots,X^{(j-1)}_{\ell^{\prime(j-1)}},X^{(j)}_{k^{(j)}},X^{(j+1)}_{\ell^{\prime(j+1)}},\dots,X^{(m)}_{\ell^{\prime(m)}})\right)\right],

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2m-1}}\sum_{r=1}^{m}\sum_{|\mathbf{S}|=r}\mathbb{E}(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{S}},\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{S}^{c}}))(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{S}},\mathbf{X}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{S}^{c}})).

where the summand in the last expression equals to 0 whenever

(iii)

|\mathbf{S}|=1
, namely,
\ell^{(j)}\neq\ell^{\prime(j)}
, for every
j\in[m]
, once again due to the marginal feasibility constraints (
7
) of the multimarginal Schrödinger system.

So we can bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{\mathbf{f}}^{*})-\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2N^{2m-1}}\exp\left(\frac{(2m+2)\|c\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon}\right)\sum_{r=2}^{m}\sum_{|\mathbf{S}|=r}\mathbb{E}(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{S}},\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{S}^{c}}))(1-p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{X}_{\mathbf{S}},\mathbf{X}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{S}^{c}}))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2N^{2m-1}}\sum_{r=2}^{m}r\binom{m}{r}N^{r}(N(N-1))^{(m-r)}e^{\frac{(4m+4)\|c\|_{\infty}}{\varepsilon}}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\frac{1}{N}.

Thus,

\sqrt{N}\left[\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\hat{\mathbf{f}}^{*})-\widehat{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}^{*})\right]\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}0.

(75)

Combining (
73
), (
75
) and Slutsky’s Lemma, the proof is complete.
∎

F
Weak limit of expectation under entropic optimal transport coupling

This section is primarily concerned with proving Theorem
4.6
. For better readability, we describe the main structure of the argument here, while the more technical components are provided in the referenced sections.

In the remainder of the proof, to simplify the notation, we assume, without loss of generality,
\mathcal{X}_{j}=[-1,1]^{d}
for
j\in[m]
. Also, we write
\mathbb{C}(\bm{x}):=\exp\left({-\frac{c(\bm{x})}{\varepsilon}}\right)
. At the same time, due to the specifics of multimarginal Schrödinger system, we use the quotient space

\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}:=\mathcal{C}^{1}/\sim,

with
\mathcal{C}^{1}=\prod_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathcal{X}_{j})
.
Now we consider the map

\widehat{\bm{T}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}

defined for
j\in[m]
and
x_{j}\in[-1,1]^{d}
as

\displaystyle\widehat{T}_{j}(\bm{\phi})(x_{j}):=

\displaystyle\quad\varepsilon\log\left(\int_{\mathcal{X}_{-j}}e^{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{m}\phi_{i}(x_{i})-c(x_{j},\,x_{-j})}{\varepsilon}}\,d\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-j}(x_{-j})\right).

The corresponding empirical Schrödinger potential
[\hat{\bm{f}}^{*}]=(\hat{f}^{*}_{1},\dots,\hat{f}^{*}_{m})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
associated with
(\hat{\nu}^{N}_{1},\dots,\hat{\nu}^{N}_{m})
solves the empirical Schrödinger system

\widehat{\bm{T}}([\hat{\bm{f}}^{*}])=0.

Similarly, the map

\bm{T}:\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}

is defined for
j\in[m]
and
x_{j}\in[-1,1]^{d}
as

\displaystyle T_{j}(\bm{\phi})(x_{j}):=

\displaystyle\quad\varepsilon\log\left(\int_{\mathcal{X}_{-j}}e^{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{m}\phi_{i}(x_{i})-c(x_{j},\,x_{-j})}{\varepsilon}}\,d\nu_{-j}(x_{-j})\right).

The corresponding population Schrödinger potential
[\bm{f}^{*}]=(f^{*}_{1},\dots,f^{*}_{m})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
associated with
(\nu_{1},\dots,\nu_{m})
solves the population Schrödinger system

\bm{T}([\bm{f}^{*}])=0.

Step 1: Linearization of the empirical EMOT system: preliminary step
.

Lemma F.1
.

Under standard assumptions,

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}\left([\bm{f}^{*}]-[\hat{\bm{f}}^{*}]\right)-\varepsilon\mathfrak{L}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\frac{d\log N}{N}\bigr),

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}:=\begin{pmatrix}B_{-1}(\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-1}-\nu_{-1})\\
\vdots\\
B_{-m}(\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-m}-\nu_{-m})\end{pmatrix}

(76)

with

\displaystyle B_{-i}(\hat{\nu}_{-i}^{N}-\nu_{-i}):=\int p_{\varepsilon}^{*}(x_{i},x_{-i})d(\hat{\nu}_{-i}^{N}-\nu_{-i})(x_{-i}).

(77)

Lemma F.2
.

Under standard assumptions,

\displaystyle\|B_{-i}(\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-i}-\nu_{-i})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{N}}\right).

(78)

The proof of Lemma
F.1
and Lemma
F.2
could be found in Appendix
G
.

Step 2: Constructing auxiliary operators to approximate empirical operators
.

Define for
i\neq j
,
D_{ij}:\mathcal{C}^{1}([-1,1]^{d})\to\mathcal{C}^{1}([-1,1]^{d})
as

\displaystyle(D_{ij}f_{j})(x_{i})=\int f_{j}(x_{j})p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(x_{i},x_{-i})d\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-i}(x_{-i}).

We could consider
\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}:\mathcal{C}^{1}\to\mathcal{C}^{1}

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}\\
\vdots\\
f_{m}\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}\\
\vdots\\
f_{m}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{k\neq 1}D_{1k}f_{k}\\
\vdots\\
\sum_{k\neq m}D_{mk}f_{k}\end{pmatrix}:=(\text{I}+\mathbb{D})\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}\\
\vdots\\
f_{m}\end{pmatrix}.

(79)

Step 3: Bridging auxiliary operators and population operators
.

The Fréchet derivative
\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
of
\widehat{\bm{T}}
could be computed as

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}\\
\vdots\\
f_{m}\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}\\
\vdots\\
f_{m}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{k\neq 1}A_{1k}f_{k}\\
\vdots\\
\sum_{k\neq m}A_{mk}f_{k}\end{pmatrix}:=(\text{I}+\mathbb{A})\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}\\
\vdots\\
f_{m}\end{pmatrix},

(80)

where
A_{ij}:\mathcal{C}^{1}([-1,1]^{d})\to\mathcal{C}^{1}([-1,1]^{d})
is defined for
i\neq j
as

\displaystyle(A_{ij}f_{j})(x_{i})=\int f_{j}(x_{j})\hat{p}_{\varepsilon}(x_{i},x_{-i})d\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-i}(x_{-i}).

It is easily observed that
\text{I}+\mathbb{A}
is the Fréchet derivative
\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
of
\widehat{\bm{T}}
.
Define for
i\neq j
,
H_{ij}:\mathcal{C}^{1}([-1,1]^{d})\to\mathcal{C}^{1}([-1,1]^{d})
as

\displaystyle(H_{ij}f_{j})(x_{i})=\int f_{j}(x_{j})p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(x_{i},x_{-i})d\nu_{-i}(x_{-i}).

We could consider
\bm{\Gamma}:\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}\\
\vdots\\
f_{m}\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}\\
\vdots\\
f_{m}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{k\neq 1}H_{1k}f_{k}\\
\vdots\\
\sum_{k\neq m}H_{mk}f_{k}\end{pmatrix}:=(\text{I}+\mathbb{H})\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}\\
\vdots\\
f_{m}\end{pmatrix}.

(81)

It is also easily observed that
\text{I}+\mathbb{H}
is the Fréchet derivative
\bm{\Gamma}:\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
of
\bm{T}
.

Step 4: Operator invertibility and operator norm bounds
.

As demonstrated by Lemma 3.2 in
[
21
]
, one could leverage Fredholm Alternative Theorem to show that
\bm{\Gamma}(\cdot)
is an invertible linear self-map of
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
satisfying

\left\|\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}\right\|\leq C,

for some constant
C
depending on the marginals
\nu_{1},\dots,\nu_{m}
and the cost function
c
. Now we consider the operator norm bound of
\|\mathbb{A}-\mathbb{H}\|
.

Lemma F.3
.

For any
i,j\in[m]
with
i\neq j
, we have that

\|\mathbb{A}-\mathbb{H}\|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left((m-1)\,\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{N}}+(m-1)^{2}\,N^{-1/(2\vee d)}(\log N)^{\mathbf{1}_{\{d=2\}}}\right).

(82)

Step 5: Linearization of the empirical EMOT system: final step
.

This section is to conclude the linearization of the empirical EMOT system and thus establish the building block of the weak limit proof, given in the following lemma.

Lemma F.4
.

Under standard assumptions, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left([\bm{f}^{*}]-[\hat{\bm{f}}^{*}]\right)-\varepsilon\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}\mathfrak{L}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}\left(N^{-1/2}\right).

Step 6: Decomposition of
\sqrt{N}\left(\hat{\pi}^{N}_{\varepsilon}(g)-\pi^{*}_{\varepsilon}(g)\right)
.

\sqrt{N}\left(\hat{\pi}^{N}_{\varepsilon}(g)-\pi_{\varepsilon}^{*}(g)\right)=\sqrt{N}\int g\,(d\hat{\pi}^{N}_{\varepsilon}-d\pi^{*}_{\varepsilon}):=A+B+\sum_{j=1}^{m}C_{j}+D.

(83)

Here,

A:=\sqrt{N}\int g(\bm{x})e^{-\frac{c(\bm{x})}{\varepsilon}}(e^{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{m}\hat{f}^{*}_{i}(x_{i})}{\varepsilon}}-e^{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{m}f^{*}_{i}(x_{i})}{\varepsilon}})d(\otimes_{k=1}^{m}\nu_{k})(\bm{x}).

B:=\sqrt{N}\int g(\bm{x})e^{-\frac{c(\bm{x})}{\varepsilon}}(e^{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{m}\hat{f}^{*}_{i}(x_{i})}{\varepsilon}}-e^{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{m}f^{*}_{i}(x_{i})}{\varepsilon}})d\left(\otimes_{k=1}^{m}\hat{\nu}^{N}_{k}-\otimes_{k=1}^{m}\nu_{k}\right)(\bm{x}).

To lighten the notation, for
\bm{r}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{m})\in\{0,1\}^{m}
, we write
\tilde{\nu}_{r_{j}}=\nu_{j}
if
r_{j}=0
and
\tilde{\nu}_{r_{j}}=\hat{\nu}^{N}_{j}-\nu_{j}
if
r_{j}=1
.
\sharp\bm{r}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}r_{j}
. We define
\tilde{\bm{\nu}}_{\bm{r}}:=\otimes\tilde{\nu}_{r_{j}}
. For example,
\tilde{\bm{\nu}}_{\bm{r}}=(\hat{\nu}_{1}^{N}-\nu_{1})\otimes\nu_{2}\otimes\dots\otimes\nu_{m}
if
\bm{r}=(1,0,\dots,0)
,
\tilde{\bm{\nu}}_{\bm{r}}=(\hat{\nu}_{1}^{N}-\nu_{1})\otimes\nu_{2}\otimes(\hat{\nu}_{3}^{N}-\nu_{3})\otimes\nu_{4}\dots\otimes\nu_{m}
if
\bm{r}=(1,0,1,0\dots,0)
.

C_{j}:=\sqrt{N}\int g(\bm{x})e^{-\frac{c(\bm{x})}{\varepsilon}}e^{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{m}f^{*}_{i}(x_{i})}{\varepsilon}}d(\nu_{1}\otimes\dots\nu_{j-1}\otimes(\hat{\nu}_{j}^{N}-\nu_{j})\otimes\nu_{j+1}\otimes\dots\otimes\nu_{m})(\bm{x}).

D:=\sum_{\sharp\bm{r}=2}^{m}\sum_{\tilde{\bm{\nu}}_{\bm{r}}}\sqrt{N}\int g(\bm{x})e^{-\frac{c(\bm{x})}{\varepsilon}}e^{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{m}f^{*}_{i}(x_{i})}{\varepsilon}}d\tilde{\bm{\nu}}_{\bm{r}}(\bm{x}).

Lemma
J.5
gives that

A=\frac{\sqrt{N}}{\varepsilon}\int g(\bm{x})e^{\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{m}f^{*}_{k}(x_{k})-c(\bm{x})}{\varepsilon}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\hat{f}^{*}_{i}(x_{i})-f^{*}_{i}(x_{i})\right)d(\otimes_{k=1}^{m}\nu_{k})(\bm{x})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Using (
F.4
), we have that
A=\bar{A}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
with

\bar{A}=-\sqrt{N}\mathop{\text{$\int$}}\nolimits\bigoplus\left(\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\displaystyle\int p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(x_{1},x_{-1})d(\hat{\nu}_{-1}^{N}-\nu_{-1})\\
\vdots\\
\displaystyle\int p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(x_{m},x_{-m})d(\hat{\nu}_{-m}^{N}-\nu_{-m})\end{pmatrix}\right)g\,p^{*}_{\varepsilon}\,\,\,d(\otimes_{k=1}^{m}\nu_{k}).

Lemma
J.6
indicates that
B=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).
Fubini’s theorem gives that

C_{j}=\sqrt{N}(\hat{\nu}_{j}^{N}-\nu_{j})(g^{(j)})

where
g^{(j)}:\mathcal{X}_{j}\to\mathbb{R}
is defined as
(\otimes_{k\neq j}\nu_{k})(g\,p^{*}_{\varepsilon}).

In advantage of Lemma B in Section 6.3.2 in
[
82
]
, we get that
D=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Step 7: Manipulating the multi-sample V-statistics
. Finally, we realize that the term of interest
(\ref{decomposition_four_part_CLT_coupling})
is actually a centered V-statistics plus an extra asymptotically negligible term. At the same time, it is noteworthy that term
\bar{A}
is a V-statistics of order
m
and degree
(m-1,\dots,m-1)
with a non-symmetric kernel and
C_{j}
is a U-statistics of order 1 for
j\in[m]
. To derive the weak limit of this type, we need to first adjust and symmetrize the kernel and then use U-statistics to approximate V-statistics as Lemma
J.7
. Finally we apply the CLT for multi-sample U-statistics, thanks to Theorem 4.5.1 in
[
61
]
, and then the conclusion follows. First define

\displaystyle\Psi(y^{(j)}_{\alpha},\alpha=i_{j,1},\dots,i_{j,m-1};j=1,\dots,m)

\displaystyle:=-\mathop{\text{$\int$}}\nolimits\bigoplus\left(\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(x_{1},y^{(2)}_{i_{2,1}},\dots,y^{(m)}_{i_{m,1}})-1\\
p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(y^{(1)}_{i_{1,1}},x_{2},\dots,y^{(m)}_{i_{m,2}})-1\\
\vdots\\
p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(y^{(1)}_{i_{1,m-2}},y^{(2)}_{i_{2,m-2}},\dots,x_{m-1},y^{(m)}_{i_{m,m-1}})-1\\
p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(y^{(1)}_{i_{1,m-1}},y^{(2)}_{i_{2,m-1}},\dots,y^{(m-1)}_{i_{m-1,m-1}},x_{m})-1\end{pmatrix}\right)g(\bm{x})p^{*}_{\varepsilon}(\bm{x})d(\otimes_{k=1}^{m}\nu_{k})(\bm{x}).

To symmetrize the kernel above, we further define

\displaystyle\quad\Psi^{\text{sym}}(y^{(j)}_{\alpha},\alpha=i_{j,1},\dots,i_{j,m-1};j=1,\dots,m)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left((m-1)!\right)^{m}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi_{j}\in S_{m-1}\\
1\leq j\leq m\end{subarray}}\Psi(y^{(j)}_{\beta},\beta=i_{j,\pi_{j}(1)},\dots,i_{j,\pi_{j}(m-1)};j=1,\dots,m),

where
S_{m-1}
denotes all the permutation on
\{1,\dots,m-1\}
. Finally, we define

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{K}(y^{(j)}_{\beta},\beta=i_{j,1},\dots,i_{j,m-1};j=1,\dots,m)

\displaystyle=\Psi^{\text{sym}}(y^{(j)}_{\beta},\beta=i_{j,1},\dots,i_{j,m-1};j=1,\dots,m)+\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{t=1}^{m-1}s_{j}(y_{i_{j,t}}^{(j)})

with
s_{j}(\cdot)=(\nu_{-j})(g\,p^{*}_{\varepsilon})(\cdot)-(\otimes_{k=1}^{m}\nu_{k})(g\,p^{*}_{\varepsilon})
. One could verify that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}(\bar{A}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}C_{j})=\frac{1}{N^{m(m-1)}}\sum_{\flat}\mathcal{K}(X^{(j)}_{\beta},\beta=i_{j,1},\dots,i_{j,m-1};j=1,\dots,m):=V_{N},

where
\sum_{\flat}
denotes the summation over all indices
1\leq i_{j,k}\leq N,\,1\leq k\leq m-1,\,j=1,\dots,m.
It is noteworthy that
V_{N}
is centered here due to the multimarginal Schrödinger system and the fact that the precomposition of a centered random
variable with a bounded linear operator is again centered. Thus, let us define

\displaystyle U_{N}:=\frac{1}{\tbinom{N}{m-1}^{m}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i_{j,1}<\dots<i_{j,m-1}\leq N\\
\,j=1,\dots,m\end{subarray}}\mathcal{K}(X^{(j)}_{\beta},\beta=i_{j,1},\dots,i_{j,m-1};j=1,\dots,m).

By Theorem 4.5.1 in
[
61
]
, we get that

\displaystyle\sqrt{N}U_{N}\overset{w}{\to}\mathcal{N}(0,\bar{\sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}(g)),

where

\displaystyle\bar{\sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}(g)=(m-1)^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\text{Var}(\phi^{(j)}(X_{1}^{(j)})),

with

\displaystyle\phi^{(j^{*})}(x)=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{K}(X^{(j)}_{\beta},\beta=1,\dots,m-1,j\in[m]\backslash\{j^{*}\};x,X^{(j^{*})}_{2},\dots,X^{(j^{*})}_{m-1})\right],

for
m
independent sequences
X_{1}^{(j)},\dots,X_{m-1}^{(j)}\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\nu_{j}
,
j\in[m]
of independent random variables. Finally, Slutsky’s Lemma and Lemma
J.7
concludes the proof.

∎

Define for
j\in[m]

b_{j}(x_{j})=\int g(x_{j},x_{-j})p_{\varepsilon}^{*}(x_{j},x_{-j})\,d\nu_{-j}(x_{-j})-\pi_{\varepsilon}^{*}(g).

Also define the tuple of functions

q^{j,y}=(q_{1}^{j,y},\ldots,q_{m}^{j,y})
by

q_{i}^{j,y}(x_{i}):=\begin{cases}0,&i=j,\\[5.0pt]
\displaystyle\int p_{\varepsilon}^{*}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})\prod_{k\notin\{i,j\}}d\nu_{k}(x_{k})\biggr|_{x_{j}=y}-1,&i\neq j.\end{cases}

The multimarginal Schrödinger system (
7
) yields that

\int q_{i}^{j,y}(x_{i})d\nu_{i}(x_{i})=0
for all
i\in[m]
and
\int q_{i}^{j,y}(x_{i})\,d\nu_{j}(y)=0
.
For notational simplicity, we define the operator
\bigoplus:\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}\to\mathcal{C}^{1}(\mathcal{X})
by
\bigoplus[u](\bm{x})=\sum_{j=1}^{m}u_{j}(x_{j})
.
We claim that

(m-1)\bar{\phi}^{(j)}(y)=b_{j}(y)-\pi_{\varepsilon}^{*}\!\left(g\,\bigoplus(\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}[q^{j,y}])\right).

(84)

Actually, this follows from the computation of the first-order projections

\bar{\phi}^{(j)}(y)=\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{K}\mid X_{1}^{(j)}=y]

of the kernel
\mathcal{K}

defining the variance in Theorem
4.6
.
The second marginal fluctuation term
\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\sum_{t=1}^{m-1}b_{k}(X_{t}^{(k)})\mid X_{1}^{(j)}=y\bigr]
equals
b_{j}(y)/(m-1)
due to independence.
As for the first term, let
R^{\bm{\tau}}
be the input tuple to
\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}

in the summand of
\Psi
corresponding to

\bm{\tau}\in S_{m-1}^{m}
.
For
i\neq j
, denote by
r_{ij}
the position of the
j
-th marginal
observation in row
i
.
Independence and the multimarginal Schrödinger system (
7
) give,
for
\nu_{j}
-almost every
y
,

\mathbb{E}\!\left[R_{i}^{\bm{\tau}}(x_{i})\,\middle|\,X_{1}^{(j)}=y\right]=\begin{cases}0,&i=j,\\
\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau_{j}(r_{ij})=1\}}q_{i}^{j,y}(x_{i}),&i\neq j.\end{cases}

Since

|\{\tau_{j}\in S_{m-1}:\tau_{j}(r_{ij})=1\}|=(m-2)!

and
q_{j}^{j,y}=0
, averaging yields

\mathbb{E}\,\biggl[\frac{1}{((m-1)!)^{m}}\sum_{\bm{\tau}\in S_{m-1}^{m}}R^{\bm{\tau}}\,\biggm|\,X_{1}^{(j)}=y\biggr]=\frac{q^{j,y}}{m-1}.

Passing this conditional expectation through the bounded linear
maps in
\Psi
, we know that the first term contributes the
-\frac{1}{m-1}\pi_{\varepsilon}^{*}\!\left(g\,\bigoplus\!\left(\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}[q^{j,y}]\right)\right)
term.
Consequently, the asymptotic variance term in the theorem equals

\displaystyle\bar{\sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}(g)

\displaystyle=(m-1)^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\operatorname{Var}_{\nu_{j}}(\bar{\phi}^{(j)})

(85)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int\left[b_{j}(y)-\pi_{\varepsilon}^{*}\!\left(g\,\bigoplus\!\left(\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}[q^{j,y}]\right)\right)\right]^{2}\,d\nu_{j}(y).

Suppose first that
\bar{\sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}(g)=0
. Then for
\nu_{j}
-almost every
y
,

b_{j}(y)=\pi_{\varepsilon}^{*}\!\left(g\,\bigoplus\!\left(\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}[q^{j,y}]\right)\right).

Let
u=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{m})
be any
\mathcal{C}^{1}
tuple such that

\nu_{j}(u_{j})=0
for every
j\in[m]
. The
i
-th component of

\sum_{j}\int q^{j,y}u_{j}(y)\,d\nu_{j}(y)
is

\sum_{j\neq i}\int u_{j}(x_{j})p_{\varepsilon}^{*}(x_{i},x_{-i})\,d\nu_{-i}(x_{-i}).

Then in the quotient space
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{1}
, with
[\cdot]
denoting the equivalence class,

\biggl[\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int q^{j,y}u_{j}(y)\,d\nu_{j}(y)\biggr]=\bm{\Gamma}[u]-[u].

(86)

Hence, for the bounded linear operator
[v]\mapsto\pi_{\varepsilon}^{*}\,\bigl(g\,\bigoplus\!\left(\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}[v]\bigr)\right)
on the quotient space
\widetilde{C}^{1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{j}(b_{j}u_{j})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int u_{j}(y)\,\pi_{\varepsilon}^{*}\,\bigl(g\,\bigoplus\!\left(\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}[q^{j,y}]\bigr)\right)\,d\nu_{j}(y)

\displaystyle=\pi_{\varepsilon}^{*}\,\biggl(g\,\bigoplus\,\Bigl(\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}\Bigl[\sum_{j=1}^{m}\int q^{j,y}u_{j}(y)\,d\nu_{j}(y)\Bigr]\Bigr)\biggr)

\displaystyle=\pi_{\varepsilon}^{*}\!\left(g\,\bigoplus\,\bigl(\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}\bigl(\bm{\Gamma}[u]-[u]\bigr)\bigr)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\nu_{j}(b_{j}u_{j})-\pi_{\varepsilon}^{*}\!\left(g\,\bigoplus(\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}[u])\right).

This implies that
\pi_{\varepsilon}^{*}\!\left(g\,\bigoplus(\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}[u])\right)=0.

Since
q^{j,y}
is a componentwise centered
\mathcal{C}^{1}
tuple, take
u=q^{j,y}
and then equation (
84
) yields
b_{j}(y)=0
for
\nu_{j}
-a.e.
y
.

Conversely, suppose that
b_{j}=0
,
\nu_{j}
-almost surely, for
j\in[m]
. Choose any representative
u
of

\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}[q^{j,y}]
.
The quotient identity
\bm{\Gamma}[u]=[q^{j,y}]
means that there are
constants
c_{i}
, with
\sum_{i}c_{i}=0
, such that

u_{i}(x_{i})+\sum_{k\neq i}\int u_{k}(x_{k})p_{\varepsilon}^{*}(x_{i},x_{-i})\,d\nu_{-i}(x_{-i})=q_{i}^{j,y}(x_{i})+c_{i}.

Integrating the equation above against
\nu_{i}
, the multimarginal Schrödinger system (
7
) yields that

\sum_{k=1}^{m}\nu_{k}(u_{k})=c_{i},

and therefore

m\sum_{k=1}^{m}\nu_{k}(u_{k})=\sum_{i=1}^{m}c_{i}=0.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\pi_{\varepsilon}^{*}\!\left(g\,\bigoplus\!\left(\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}[q^{j,y}]\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\int u_{i}(x_{i})\left[\int g(x_{i},x_{-i})p_{\varepsilon}^{*}(x_{i},x_{-i})\,d\nu_{-i}(x_{-i})\right]d\nu_{i}(x_{i})

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\nu_{i}\!\left(u_{i}\bigl(b_{i}+\pi_{\varepsilon}^{*}(g)\bigr)\right)=\pi_{\varepsilon}^{*}(g)\sum_{i=1}^{m}\nu_{i}(u_{i})=0.

Then equation (
84
) gives that
\bar{\sigma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}(g)=0
.
∎

G
Technical details for Linearization of the empirical EMOT system

The purpose of this section is to present the detailed proofs of Lemma
F.1
and Lemma
F.2
. We also include several technical results that will recur in other sections. The proofs of the two lemmas are given first, followed by a detailed discussion of the supporting arguments.

The proof is carried out in two steps, with all technical details collected in Appendix
G
. The first step, conducted in
subsection
G
,
is to establish Lemma
G.2
, implying that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\bm{T}}([\bm{f}^{*}])-\widehat{\bm{T}}([\hat{\bm{f}}^{*}])-\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}([\bm{f}^{*}]-[\hat{\bm{f}}^{*}])\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\|[\bm{f}^{*}]-[\hat{\bm{f}}^{*}]\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}^{2}\right)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{d\log N}{N}\right),

where the last equality is implied by Lemma
4.8
.
The second step, which is detailed in subsection
G
, starts from noting that

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{T}}([\hat{\bm{f}}^{*}])=\bm{T}([\bm{f}^{*}])=0.

So we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\bm{T}}([\bm{f}^{*}])-\bm{T}([\bm{f}^{*}])-\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}([\bm{f}^{*}]-[\hat{\bm{f}}^{*}])\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{d\log N}{N}\right).

(87)

Lemma
G.5
, as the main lemma in subsection
G

establishes that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\bm{T}}([\bm{f}^{*}])-\bm{T}([\bm{f}^{*}])-\varepsilon\mathfrak{L}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\|[\bm{f}^{*}]-[\hat{\bm{f}}^{*}]\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}^{2}\right)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{d\log N}{N}\right).

(88)

The proof is complete combining (
87
) and (
88
) together.
∎

It is enough to show that, for all
i\in[m]
,

\displaystyle\|B_{-i}(\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-i}-\nu_{-i})\|_{\infty}=\sup_{x_{i}}|B_{-i}(\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-i}-\nu_{-i})|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{N}}\right),

as well as

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x_{i}}B_{-i}(\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-i}-\nu_{-i})\|_{\infty}=\sup_{x_{i}}\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i,k}}B_{-i}(\hat{\nu}^{N}_{-i}-\nu_{-i})\right|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\log N}{N}}\right).

These two asymptotic equality comes as a corollary of Lemma
G.6
.
∎

The subsequent discussion provides additional details.

Step 1. Building Fréchet differentiability

We first briefly recapitulate the settings along with notations from Section
F
as follows.
Let
m\geq 2
and
\mathcal{X}_{j}\subset[-1,1]^{d}
be compact sets.
For each
j
, let
\nu_{j}
be a Borel probability on
\mathcal{X}_{j}
,
and
\widehat{\nu}_{j}^{N}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\delta_{X_{k}^{(j)}}

the empirical measure of
N
i.i.d. samples from
\nu_{j}
.
Write the empirical product over all coordinates except
i
as

\widehat{\nu}_{-i}^{\,N}:=\bigotimes_{j\neq i}\widehat{\nu}_{j}^{N}
.
Let
c\in\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathcal{X})
and set
\mathbb{C}=e^{-c/\varepsilon}
.
